                                        PREFACE

  Surat: The city located at on the coast of the Arabian Sea where the Tapti River (Tapi)
passes from the middle of the city and various other creeks passes through southern part of
the city which merges into the sea. Looking at the geographical location of the city, the city
is prone to natural calamities like river/khadi flood, heavy rainfalls and cyclones. Keeping
the motto “Surat: The city that always cares” in mind, Surat Municipal Corporation has
contributed to the development of the city by providing not only the basic amenities but also
the best in class infrastructure and services.

   Along with the development of the city, the population of the city has also increased. With
the increase in population there are possibilities of natural disasters and outbreak of disease.
The impact of Global Warming and Climate Change can also contribute or cause disaster.

  Considering the ideology of “Precaution is better than cure”, Surat Municipal Corpo-
ration is publishing the Disaster Management & Preparedness Plan since 2002. Disaster
Management Plan of SMC consists of the guidelines and action plan for the activities to be
carried out before, during and after the disaster covering various activities like rescue and
relief work including relocating and rehabilitation. The aim of preparing the disaster man-
agement and preparedness plan is to reduce the response time in the event of any disaster.
Such measures can help avoid human loss and reduce damages to properties. The DMP is
updated with latest information every year.

  This Disaster Management and Preparedness Plan also includes advanced planning and
preventive measures in the event of natural disasters and outbreak of diseases. The plan
contains information regarding the DO’s and DON’Ts as a part of precautionary measures
and information regarding first aid treatment for various disasters. The DMP also enlists
the specific roles and responsibilities of employees/officers in case of disaster. Necessary
training has been provided to the concern in this regard. Meetings and trainings area also
organized with a view to increase people participation and to make them aware about the
precautionary steps to be taken. Zone level and ward level disaster management plan is
also prepared considering the decentralized administration.

  We hope that this planning serves its purpose for the city.



  We welcome your sincere suggestions to update this Disaster Management Plan.


  M. Nagarajan IAS                                   Mayaben Dharmendrabhai Mavani

                              Terminologis for Tropical Cyclone
   The classification adopted by India Meterollogical Department to classify such disturbances is based
on maximum strength of sustained wind in the circulation.

                       Type of Disturbance                                       Associated wind speed
                         Low Pressure area                                          Less than 17 Kts.
                             Depression                                                17-27 kts.
                          Deep Depression                                              28-33 kts.
                           Cyclonic Storm                                              34-47 kts.
                       Sever Cyclonic Storm                                            48-63 kts.
                     Very severe Cyclonic Storm                                      64-119 kts. kts.
                       Super Cyclonic Storm                                        120 kts. and above




                                                     (1 knot = 1.85 kmph)


                 Expected Wind Speed                                           Expected damage
 60-90 kmph                                                       Tree branches broken off, some damage to
                                                                  kachchaa house.
 90-120 kmph                                                      Tree uprooted, pakka houses damaged, communi-
                                                                  cation distrupted.
 More than 120 kmph                                               Big tree uprooted, Wind spread damage to houses
                                                                  and installation. Total distruption of communication.

                                                 CHAPTER - 3
                                       The outlines of Rains / Floods at Surat.
    Surat city is situated between 20.15’ and 22.28’ Longitudes and between 72.28’ and 74.23’ East Longitudes in
Surat District area of South Gujarat. The area of Surat District is 462.149 Sq. Kms. According to 2011 census, the
population of Surat District is 49,95,174 and that of Surat city is 46,45,384.
    Kakrapar weir and Ukai dam are the two major dams constructed in 1953 and 1972 AD respectively on river Tapi.
The History of Cyclones:
    Surat district has coastal bank of 83.00 Kms & 26 villages having population of 66,781 are located on this sea
coast. Occasionally Surat city has experienced heavy rains. But during the near past, it has no major impact of
cyclone. Of course on the basis of historical records, the city suffered a big cyclone in 1782. It had taken a toll of a
large nos. of old and new building at Burhanpuri gate. (There was no heavy toll of human life) The islands of Tapi
were also submerged by it and 2000 people died. There was a heavy damage on the coastal areas also. Other
Cyclones were experienced by Surat in the year of 1800 & 1872 too. But the impact of cyclone was comparatively
less in Surat.
Details of rains:
    Heavy rain fall is registered in Surat frequently during monsoon season in the past. According to the study of
metrological department, there is possibility of flood even in case of little rain as the catchments area of the upstream
portion of river Tapi is too large. Obviously, heavy rains increase the possibility of heavy floods. Rains in the city
are also a cause of flood situation.

1968 Flood:
    The heaviest flood in the river Tapi occurred in the year 1968. At that time 15.60 lakh cusecs water
was discharged from Kakarapar weir on the river Tapi. The highest water level in river Tapi at Surat Hope
Bridge was 103.50 feet. During the time of high tide in the sea the hike in water level recorded was up
to 12 feet. Due to this flood, situation remained disturbed for a long period of time. During the period of
30 hrs. 25 inches of rain in Ukai and 15 inches of rain in Surat were recorded. The total area of 1360
sq.kms was flooded resulting into major losses/damages to life and property. The river overflowed at
Mandvi, Baudhan, Kathor and Nana Varachha villages. Between Ahmedabad & Mumbai broad gauge
railway flooded and was submerged under water. Surat City and Udhna industrial area had suffered from
damages on a large scale.
    Surat is situated on the banks of river Tapi & Arabian Sea is 15 km away from the city. Surat had faced
natural calamities like cyclone and floods in the past, as the river Tapi passes through the city. Surat
Municipal Corporation has taken precautionary measures in the past to protect the city from the damages
caused by such natural calamities.
    After 1968 flood, long term planning has been made for the protection against flood. This includes:
    (1) Ukai dam was constructed in the year 1972 and due to these 8.40 lakh cusecs of flood water was
put under control.
    (2) Tapi flood protection project was sanctioned in the year 1971 by the planning commission & was
revised and sanctioned in the year 1993.

Flood in the year 1994, 1998 & 2002:
    After the construction of Ukai dam, flood was partially controlled. In the year 1994 and 1998 floods release
of water was 5.25 lakh cusecs and 7.00 lakh cusecs respectively. Incomplete work of sluice regulators and
embankments at Chhaprabhatha, Variav and Tunki were the main reasons for the entry of flood water in the city.
    Due to flood in 1998, 23 villages and population of 14.50 lakh residing in 245.59 sq.km areas were ad-
versely affected. Out of these Rander, Katargam and Umra areas of the city were worst affected. In the year
1998, release of 7 lakh cusecs of water, highest flood level of 11.40 meters (101.30 feet) was recorded at
Hope Bridge on the river Tapi. Due to release of 3.25 lakhs cusecs of water, highest level of 8.20 meters
(91.94 feet) was recorded at Hope Bridge of the river Tapi in the year 2002.
    It has therefore, emerged that advance planning and effective long term measures for the prevention of
flood is very necessary, which includes preparation of flood maps of prime areas and assessment of flood
work.
Flood of August 2006 (6-8-06 to 11-8-06):
    Owing to the inflow of water from the catchment area in the Ukai dam in August-2006, water was dis-
charged on large scale from the dam which was comparatively more than the inflow of water discharged

                                                     )10 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
in previous years. Actually, there was a release of 6.0 to 7.50 lakh cusecs of water continuously for 13
hours and 8.0 to 9.5 lakh cusecs of water continuously for 42 hours 128 s.q.kms area of the city out of
326 s.q.kms had the impact of flood.
                                     Details of flood in river Tapi.
   The lower area of the Tapi river basin is frequently affected by floods & generally it is seen that there
is a possibility of flood at an interval of four years. Surat city had suffered from 10 (ten) heavy floods in
19th century (1801-1900) and 19 (nineteen) heavy floods in 20th century (1901-2000). These floods have
taken a heavy toll of life and property.

                                               CHAPTER - 4
                                Surat Municipal Corporation Public Request.
                About the flood warning sirens blown to warn the public about flood


   When there is a possibility of flood in the river Tapi, Siren is blown, such that the citizens of Surat get
timely information on regular basis. When the flood level in the river reaches at 7.5 meters (89.42 ft.) to
8.5 meters (96.00 ft.) at Nehru Bridge the siren blows as follows.


1. When the river level rises 7.5 meters the Siren is blown continuously for one minute.
2. When the river level rises to 8.5 meters (warning level) the Siren is blown for half a minute and again
   for half minute and again for half minute, each time after an interval of half a minute.
3. When the river level raises to 9.5 meters (danger level) the Siren is blown intermittently for 2 minute.


   Every morning the Siren is blown at 10:30 a.m. regularly. However at times of possible announce-
ment, the information will be made available to public through mobile van in addition to blowing of Siren
for warning the public.


   In the morning from 10 hours on date ......../......./....... as per the following schedule various types
of Siren is blown at the times of warning / danger level of water of river Tapi to make public acquainted
regarding situation of flood.


                       1          At 10.00 am for information of 7.5 meters (89.42 ft.) level

                       2          At 10.05 am for information of 8.5 meters (92.71 ft.) level

                       3          At 10.10 am for information of 9.5 meters (96.00 ft.) level



   Citizens are advised to note that Siren blown on........./......../........ date is only as mock drill so that
during actual emergency people can easily recognize the indication of various types of Siren blown.

                                                 CHAPTER - 5
                                Pre-Monsoon Contingency Plan (SOP)
     To cope up with possibility of heavy rains, flood and natural calamities, Standing Instructions for
                         Pre-Monsoon works to be carried out at every year.

All Departments / Zones :
1. Time schedule should be prepared for refilling / repairing of excavated trenches. Priority should be given to
     trenches on the heavy traffic roads / main roads. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / Ex.Eng. - RDD / HE / Drng. Engr.)

2.   Keep the necessary stock of fuel for dewatering pumps, generator sets, vehicles / machinery. Necessary stock
     should be provided to all Zonal office / fire stations etc by 15th June 2026. (under annexure or Patrak -11)
     ( Ex.Eng.-All ZO / Ex.Eng./ Workshop / CFO / Drng. Engr )

3.   Necessary steps should be taken immediately to remove the contamination in the drinking water due to leak-
     age of drainage / water supply lines. As part of hygienic measures, in case of contamination of water lines,
     dispatching of water tanks for providing pure / potable water should be arranged for the affected areas. Steps
     should be taken for distributing chlorine tablets and for sending medical team of the Surat Municipal Corpora-
     tion. If needed, for new area, these arrangements should also be made. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / MOH / HE /
     Drng. Engr.)

4.   Tender procedures for appointing the agency for carrying out activities related to premonsoon and during
     monsoon period & purchasing of required materials should be immediately done. ( All HOD)

5.   The excavation work carried out by Gujarat Gas Co., Reliance Telecom, Torrent Power Ltd., GSEB, Telecom
     department and other private agencies should be completed by 20th May 2026. A Zone wise list should be
     prepared for the same after survey and concerned agencies should be informed in writing to complete the
     trench filling by 30th May 2026. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / Ex.Eng. - RDD / HE / Drng. Engr.)

6.   A list containing the details of the no. & types of vehicles, their keys, names & addresses of drivers, their con-
     tact no. etc should be kept ready in all Zone offices & concerned departments. The copy of the same should
     be submitted to the Office Superintendent by 1st May 2026.( annexure - 1 ) ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone / Ex. Eng.-
     Workshop / Concerned HOD / CFD )

7. All the applications for water and gutter (drainage) connection should be checked up and procedure for approval should
   be completed. The approved works should be completed by 20th May 2026. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / HE / Drng. Engr.)

8.   In case of power failure due to heavy rains, the generator sets should be kept in working condition so that the
     sewage pumping stations and water distribution centre remain is working condition. The capacity wise informa-
     tion regarding such generators should be submitted to the Office Superintendent. (All HOD)

9.   The necessary equipment including the dewatering pumps for the disposal of water in zones including new
     areas, generator sets, petromax, portable lights, ropes etc should be arranged to be keep at fixed places. (Ex.
     Eng.- All Zone / HE / Drng.Engr.)

10. For making easy availability of swimmers for rescue operations during floods, a latest list of names, addresses telephone
    number etc of swimmers and their institutions and for areas newly included in the corporation, the same details of the
    local persons should be prepared and kept with the zonal control office and their copies should be supplied to the Office
    Superintendent and all zonal officers by 1st May 2026. ( as per anneture-6) (Ex.Eng.- All Zone/ CFO / HE )


                                                            )16 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
11. During heavy rains / floods, necessary arrangement for the distribution of food packets should be made. The
    latest details of the food packet suppliers and NGOs should be submitted to all zonal officers/ chief accountant
    / chief project officer (UCD), Food inspectors & to the Office Superintendent by 1st of May 2026.( As per an-
    nexure - 4) ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone / CFO / Chief Acct / UCD / Food Insp.)

12. Due to heavy rains / floods epidemic may start. To cope up with the situation, arrangement should be made to
    keep sufficient stock of necessary medicines and insecticides in Hospitals / Health centers. Fogging machines
    should be kept ready and procedure for purchasing new should be arranged if needed. The arrangement for
    rapid disposal of dead animals should also be made. ( MOH/ All Dy. MOH / I/C Ex.Eng - SWM / IO)

13. Generators / Petromaxes / Emergency lights should be checked to verify their working condition to avoid the
    block out at Zonal Office. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone)

14. Special Precaution should be taken to shift the generator sets & their fual from the ground floor to a level higher
    than the H.F.L. noted in year 2006. ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone )

15. For speedy contact in times of need during monsoon list of employees along with the names & addresses of
    all officers cadre wise / employees should kept ready in all the zones / departments and one copy should be
    sent to the Office Superintendent by 1st May 2026. (As Per Annexure-2) (Ex.Eng.- All Zone/ HOD )

16. The total number of required daily wages beldars (worker) for the Pre-Monsoon work should be sent to the
    Establishment department employment department. ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone / Drng. Engr. / HE )

17. Separate data for urgent work to be prepared during the monsoon & should be kept ready by concerned zones
    / departments in fixed annexure and a copy along with a soft copy (Pen Drive) should be sent to the central
    office by 1st May 2026.

18. Whatever changes / suggestions to be made in disaster management plan prepared last year should be sent
    to the central office in fixed proforma by 1st April 2026.( All HOD )

19. The excavation work for water supply / drainage pipe line works should be completed and should be made
    safe by 20th May 2026. If needed in special areas / cases, the excavation can be carried out only with the prior
    permission of Municipal Commissioner of Surat Municipal Corporation only. (All ZO / HE / Drng.Engr.)

20. The newly installed flood gates should be serviced well in time for smooth operation. Repaired certificate
    showing that they are in working condition along with the name of the operator should invariably be submitted
    by 15th May of every year. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone /HOE)

21. De-watering pumps and generator sets should be serviced & should be kept in working condition and log book
    should be properly maintained. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / HE / Drng.Engr.)

22. The central control room one at ICCC (Udhna - Magdalla Road) and separate control room in each zone should
    be started from 1st June or first rain whichever is earlier. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / CFO )

23. Fax machines installed by the zones / department for exchange of information should be repaired / serviced so
    that they can work continuously during emergent times. Enough stock of Fax rolls / additional papers should
    be kept ready. ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone)

All Zones :
1. All zones / departments should keep a ready stock of minimum 2000 bags of sand. (All ZO)

                                                         )17 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
2.   Out of the relief centers / concerned schools should be repaired, if needed. The toilet blocks, water & lighting facilities
     of the schools / centers should be checked and appropriate steps should be taken if needed. The information regarding
     these matters should be sent to the Office Superintendent by 1st may 2026. ( as per annexure-5) (Ex.Eng.- All Zone)

3.   A list of low lying areas where water accumulates due to heavy rains should be prepared and necessary steps should
     be taken to stop such accumulation. For newly included areas, such type of water accumulation had taken place in
     the past, information regarding the arrangement for its disposal in the past should be gathered and steps should be
     taken through short / long term plans for disposal of such water. (All ZO)

4.   All control rooms and relief centers in the area affected due to the heavy rains, river floods, Khadipoor should have
     updated zone maps of size 42’ X 60’ with updated information of contact Nos. / Mobile Nos. Three copies of the same
     should be sent to central office (Ex.Eng.- All Zone)

5.   Transition routes to be used during disaster from affected areas to the relief centers should be planned in advance in
     all zones. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone)

6.   Water recharging bore wells in SMC area should be checked and cleaned to make it operational. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone)

7.   A survey of the open plots where water accumulates and owners of the private plots should be notified by 15th May
     2026 for filling them and proper steps should be taken that water does not accumulate in the plots. Special precaution-
     ary measure should be taken for the newly included areas. ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone )

8.   Arrangement for regular cleaning of the garbage and mud should be made during heavy rains. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone
     )

9.   Arrangement for cleaning of creeks, nalas, drainage (Gutter) etc including these in the newly added areas should be
     made. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone)

10. Arrangement for occupying necessary spaces / place / building in new areas for installing communication means,
    wireless sets, phones etc should be made ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone / HOD )

11. Training regarding relocation should be given to volunteers to transit to safe areas during flood disaster. Group of 50
    to 100 houses should have such trained volunteer especially in slum area. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / CPO-UCD)

All Zones / Drainage Departments:
1. Storm water and Box drain should be cleaned well. The information as per the enclosed statement-A is to be submit-
     ted in every meeting regarding the length of the storm water and box drains cleaned / yet to be cleaned out of the total
     length. Supervision should be made intensive for effective cleaning. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / Drng.Eng.)

2.   The storm water drain, box drain, katcha / temporary local drains, manholes, Jalia, & half round pipes, inlet chambers
     etc should be cleaned. New frame covers (lids) should be fixed on manhole without lids and the covers which are found
     broken should be replaced. A survey of the drainage line manholes should be made and a list should be prepared.
     The information should be submitted in the weekly meetings as per statement-A. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone / Drng.Eng.)

3.   Actions should be taken to dig temporary drains at places where water accumulation usually takes places. Special
     care regarding this should be taken for the newly added areas. ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone)

4.   Action should be taken regarding purchasing / hiring heavy duty (big size) dewatering pump. (Drng.Eng./Workshop)

     gency period. ( Ex.Eng.- All Zone )

4.   The rate of rent for hiring generators should be fixed and names, addresses, phone numbers etc of concerned agen-
     cies should be sent to Office Superintendent. (Ex. Eng. HWW)

5.   The repainting of the level marking to observe the water level of Tapi at Hope Bridge, Nehru Bridge, Vivekanand law
     level Bridge, weir cum cause way and bridges on the creeks should be done. Necessary light arrangements should
     also be completed by 15th May 2026. ( Ex. Engr - Traffic / Ex. Eng - St. Light)

6.   The details / information regarding the availability of the generator sets required during power outage due to heavy
     rain, at all zones/concernded departments/division should be collected. compiled and sent by Executive Engineer
     (Streelight) to office Superintendent. ( Ex. Engr - St. Light)

All Zones / Fire Bridge Department / Public Relation Department / Watch and Ward Department :
1. Arrangements of the boats for low lying areas suffering from accumulations of water should be made for rescue
     operations. Planning in advance for location of boats during floods should be done in coordination with the zonal
     officers. Information regarding the locations and their number of boats, rafts and Sirens should be provided.
     Procedure for the approval of extra boats to be hired for rescue operations should be done. Special care should
     be taken to do the same regarding the newly annexed areas of the corporation. (Ex.Eng.- All Zone/ CFO )

2.   For rescue and relief operations during the emergent times of flood, from where and from which institution which
     type of boats and capability will be made immediately available? The detailed information regarding this should
     include the names / status / (mobile) phone no. of the control officer & senior officers of the said organization
     should be prepared & a copy of the same should be sent to the control office and the central control room. (as
     per Annexure-9 in Gujarati and English) (CFO)

3.   For shifting of all the boats to be available for rescue / relief operations in the affected area, cranes would be
     required. So information about the cranes, their size, their weight, lifting, capacity, the necessary trucks / trail-
     ers, their size and location from where it will be available and it should be prepared along with the their names,
     addresses, status (mobile) phone number etc of the control person / officers should be prepared and a copy
     of the same should be send to the central office. (as per Annexure-10 in Gujarati and English) (CFO)

4.   Actions should be taken for necessary repairing / maintenance of all Wireless sets / Walky Talky sets. Neces-
     sary Arrangments should also be made for keeping important telephones continuously in working order. ( CFO)

5.   Necessary arrangements for warning the public regarding the flood through Public address system & for blow-
     ing of sirens should be made. ( CFO / PRO / Ex.Eng.- All Zone )

6.   The public relations officer should get the information regarding, their arrangements and should prepare a coordinated
     press note under the guidance of Deputy Commissioner & send it to the press. (PRO / Ex.Eng.- All Zone )

7.   Fire brigade / workshop equipments / instruments & vehicles should be serviced / maintained to keep them in
     working condition. (CFO / Ex.Engr.- Workshop )

8.   Necessary arrangement should be made for occupying space / place / building for communication, wireless
     sets, Phones etc for new areas. (CF0)

9.   Places should be identified keeping boats in time for rescue operations to be done from the places which are
     submerged under water. ( CFO )

10. Extra batteries should be supplied as per need so that all wireless sets / walky tokies can be continuously
    operated. ( CFO )

11. Actions should be taken to place battery wall charger at zone offices and main office so that mobile phone of
    all officers / employees can be kept continuously working.( CFO )

12. To minimize the loss of life and damages to property and to ensure restoration and rehabilition.( CFO )

13. To illustrate a concise chart, listing major executive actions required in response to urban flooding( CFO )

14. To list necessary tasks for preparedness, response relief and restoration required to be undertaken by the line
    agencies and department involed.( CFO )


                                                           )21 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
15. To ensure effective integration of tasks / events of each department at every stage of the disaster managment
    process and enable continuous coordination of all action.( CFO )

16. To enable reporting of action taken by each agency / department for further review and updating of the existing
    sop from past learning.( CFO )

17. This model sop is an indicative SOP document issued for the guidance of states and UT administrations for
    preparation of thair city specific SOPs. The instructions contained in this model SOP should not be regarded
    as exhaustive of all the action that might be considered necessary.( CFO )

18. Emergency Traffic Plan - Being Familier With The Local terrain, W &W has to provide security for safe trans-
    porting of employee, resources and relief goods to and from the affected area.( CSO )

19. Detail Communication plan - Watch & Ward Has a robust and effective communication System. it should be
    used to propagate information and warning of threatening disaster Security guards / marshals who are present
    at affrcted area play important role to collect sensitive information and disseminate emergency warning and
    other information. ( CSO )

20. Search and Rescue (SAR) - The primary Function During a disaster. Security Guards / Marshals must begin
    rescue at the earlist to take relief ti the victim till specialized forces arrive. ( CSO )

21. Deployment of Resources - Watch & Ward Should Deploy Resources, Provide Safety And security to the resorces
    so that information of any emergency could be disseminated quickly and support reached at the earlist. similar
    communication with wirless has to be established in operational camps. ( CSO )

22. Prevention of Commission of cognizable offences including all offences against property, human body and
    public transquility - provide safety by active patrolling during disasters.( CSO )

23. Accommodation of persons with special meds in evacuation shelters such as small children, pregnent women
    and mothers with nursing infants etc. in a limited shelter space it is very important to provide very important
    role in this respect.( CSO )

24. Restoration of critical infrastructure - Disaster usually affects the critical infrastructure such as road, telecom-
    munication, health centers, school, electricity supply etc. W &W has to provide protection to the which are
    involved in Restoration of such infrastructure.( CSO )

25. Safe exit of the personnel involved in disaster management - After the disaster for the safe exit of the person-
    nel from outside agencies involved in management of the disaster i.e. People from NGOs, INGOs, medical
    teams etc.( CSO )

All Zones office / Municipal School Board :
1. For starting relief centers at the time of heavy rains and flood, a latest list of all the schools building along with
     the names and addresses of Principals & their details should be kept ready at zone offices and a copy of the
     same should be submitted to the Office Superintendent by 1st May 2026. A survey should be made to find out
     centre for relief work for areas newly included in the corporation. (All ZO / AO - MSB)

2.   When water submergence takes place in residential areas or at times of flood when schools are to be used
     as relief centres the information regarding the names, addresses, phone no. etc of the concerned schools
     principals should be sent to the zonal officers & office superintendent. A circular should be sent to the effect
     that at such times the principals of concerned schools remain present and are available and they do not go on

                                                          )22 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
     leave without the permission of the Administrative Officer. (All ZO / AO - MSB)

 Information to be sent by Departments / Zones :
1. For urgent work during heavy rains, cyclone, a list containing the details of the number of vehicles, their types,
     their keys, names and addresses of their drivers, their contact phone no. etc should be kept ready in all zone
     officers and concerned departments. A copy on USB pen drive of the same should be submitted to the office
     superintendent by 15th April every year.

2.   For speedy and easy availability of the employees in time of need during monsoon, a list of the name & ad-
     dresses of all cadre wise officers / employees / beldars, cleaners etc should be kept ready by 15th April every
     year in all zones / departments and a copy and a USB pen drive of the same should be submitted to the office
     superintendent.

3.   For starting relief centres at times of heavy rains & flood, a zone wise latest list of schools should be prepared
     by the Administrative Officer - MSB containing information about the names, addresses of the principals of the
     concerned schools and of the key holders and should be sent to the every zone office / and a copy along with
     a USB pen drive should be submitted to the office superintendent in 15th of April every year.

4.   During the heavy rains / floods necessary arrangements for the distribution of food packets should be made.
     The latest details of the suppliers of food packets / NGOs should be submitted all zonal officers / chief ac-
     countant / chief project officer (UCD) food inspector to the office superintendent by 15th April.

5.   All the zonal officers should arrange for the checking whether proper facilities of lights, water & toilets are avail-
     able or not at the fixed relief centres in every zone. This information and its USB pen drive should be submitted
     by them to the office superintendent by 15th April every year.

6.   For easy availability of swimmers for rescue operations during floods. Hydraulic Engineer / Chief Fire Officer /
     all Zonal Officers should prepare a latest list of the names, addresses & telephone no. etc of swimmers & their
     organization & should submit the latest list and a USB pen drive by 15th April to the office superintendent.

7.   Hydraulic Engineer should make arrangement for supplying water high tankers when water supply is disrupted
     in the city due to heavy rains. A list of containing the information regarding the number of tankers the corporation
     has as also a list of the contractors and other license holders who can supply tanker on rent with their names,
     addresses, telephone no. should be submitted by him to the central office.

8.   For easy availability of Earth mover / machinery / heavy vehicles, the names, addresses & telephone no. of
     (suppliers) contractor / organizations (industrial units Hazira Area / other contractors) should be sent Ex.Eng.
     - Workshop / chief fire officer to the office superintendent by the 15th April.

9.   The information required by the Collector’s office regarding the various safety relief equipments / vehicles
     should be submitted to the central office within scheduled time limit.

10. The details of the rescue, relief & rehabilitation should be submitted to the central control room.

11. The details should be prepared by zones / departments regarding the location of floodgates, the name of the
    operators, the location of the keys, the name and contact no. of the responsible officer etc for timely operating
    the floodgates during the floods & the same should be sent to the office superintendent by 15th April.

12. The details regarding the name of the organization, their location & contact no. of the organizations officer for
    obtaining boats & transport vehicles & to be made available during emergencies should be prepared by Chief

                                                           )23 )

      Fire Officer & should be sent to the Office Superintendent by 15th of April.

13. The Chief Fire Officer should send the information about organization from which cranes can be made available on rent
    in emergent situation in how many number & capacity & from which locations to office superintendent by 15th of April.

14. All the zones / departments should send the details regarding the no. of generators sets and their capacity to
    the office superintendent by 15th April.

15. All zones / department should submit the information regarding the capacity / no. of dewatering pumps to office
    superintendent by 15th April

                                                     CHAPTER - 6
                       Prevention of Water borne and Water related Diseases
     The Concerned department will have to submit the information in the annexed Performa regarding the precautionary
steps taken to see that water borne and water related diseases do not occur in summer and also regarding the action for
preventing the possible epidemics as per the included action plan as follows.

Section - 1

A. Health Sanitation and Solid Waste Management

All the Deputy Medical Officer of Health should carry out the following works in their zone areas.

1.   Intense cleaning of nuisance spots, garbage bins / Public Toilet Pay & Use along with their surrounding areas should
     be regularly carried out. All types of wastes should be regularly disposed off. Sufficient quantities of Insecticides should
     be there in stock in each ward office.

2.   The use and selling of Single used plastic are banned under the Environment Protection Act-1986 and The solid waste
     management rules 2016 & PWM (Plastic Waste Management) Rule 2023 Hence, arrangement should be made to
     completely stop their use by fruit / vegetable hawkers, etc. Regular checking should be carried out and necessary
     steps should be taken against those violating the rule.

3.   Arrangement should be made to stop the prevailing use of small pieces of plastic sheets / polythene by pan hawkers
     / vendors. Regular checking should be carried out and necessary steps should be taken.

4.   If the citizens in the ward areas throws the waste / garbage on roads they should be first instructed to dispose the
     waste in door to door vehicle after segregating it only. If there is no improvement in their behaviour, notice should be
     issued to them. In case, they do not pay attention to the notice, legal complaint should be registered against them in
     the municipal court.

5.   The drainage line of public toilet should never be blocked / choked up. If it is so, the blockage should be immediately
     removed and the line should be cleaned and insecticide should be sprayed in the surrounding areas also. Intensive
     supervision should be carried for the cleanliness of Pay & Use toilets & spraying of Insecticides.

6.   Shopkeeper, shopping centre, Commercial Complex, Mall etc. organizers must keep 2 dustbins, one green coloured
     for wet waste and second blue coloured for dry waste to collect segregated waste and each Shopkeeper, shopping
     centre, commercial Complex, Mall etc. When Organizers Open their Shop/Company/shopping centre/commercial
     complex should not throw garbage on road or footpath in front of their shops, they must compulsorily give the segre-
     gated waste to door to door garbage vehicle.

7.   The Complaints regarding overflow of drainage line should be resolved by the nearby ward-office. If there is a dam-
     age in the main drainage line then the work in should be done in collaboration with the pumping station. If drainage
     chamber/line/down take pipe are damaged then the responsible person should given the notice as per the BPMC Act.

8.   Where the Drainage line is not available and complaints regarding overflow of soak pits / septic tanks are received,
     than it should be registered regularly. Such complaints should be appropriately attended and immediate actions should
     be taken for timely discharging the soiled water.

9.   The Water storage tanks of the public places such as municipal and private schools, colleges, universities, bus sta-


                                                             )30 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
    tion, religious places, commercial organization, marriage hall, multi storied building, etc. should be regularly checked.
    Written notice should also be issued to their management committees / property holders for taking necessary steps
    to keep the water potable. Arrangement should be made to see that areas around these places/ organizations are
    kept clean. The concerned employees / representatives should also be instructed to submit a certificate showing the
    details of the dates of cleaning of their water tanks and use of bleaching powder.

10. Daily each ward office should take 10 water samples from the domestic water supply line and it should be tested for
    residual chlorine with the help of chronoscope provided to the ward offices. Out of these 5 samples should be taken
    from fixed locations such as public stand post, slum areas etc. and the remaining 5 samples should be taken from
    random locations. If the amount of residual chlorine is found less than the limit prescribed by GPCB or nil, the Prosecu-
    tion Inspector & Hydraulic Engineer should be directly informed. Daily testing of water of such location should carry
    out till the results are satisfactory. Chlorine tables should be distributed to maintain the residual chlorine concentration
    up to the limit prescribed.

11. In the ward areas, if any leakage / contamination of water supply are found, the concerned department should be
    informed and repair work should be carried out immediately & a report as per the Performa should be made.

   Complaints of water leakage/contaminated water / turbid and odorous water should be immediately attended.

   Detection of leakage & repairing work of the water line should be carried out on war footing.

   Supplying drinking water tanker in the affected areas. Potable Water should be supplied by tankers till the affected
    areas leakage / contamination of water problem is solved.

   Water line in the areas of complaints regarding contaminated water should be instantly disconnected and should be
    taken care to see that contaminated water is not supplied.

   A register should be kept for the leakage / contaminated water complaints and repairing of the water line. The day to
    day information of the same should be saved in the computer. Responsible employee and officer should check the
    register daily.

   The samples of water should also be sent for bacteriological / viral analyses. A copy of report should be sent to office
    in charge.

   Arrangement should be made to repair water line leakage immediately & proper check should be carried out so that
    the repair work may continue round-the-clock when needed.

12. Arrangement should be made for the use of chlorine tablets for the drinking water supplied to public through parabs.

13. The use of drinking water pouches and drinking water bottles without BIS mark should be completely prohibited.

14. Arrangement be made to destroy the soiled & decayed fruits sold in ward areas.

15. Lorries selling foods/eatables :-

15.1.Checking of Lorries selling foods/eatables should be made intensive, water samples of these Lorries should be col-
     lected and sent to laboratory for analysis.

15.2.The Lorries selling foods/eatables should be forced to cover the items with fly protecting nets.

15.3.Care should be taken to keep the space around the food Lorries clean.

15.4.The use of disposable glasses & plates (other than prohibited plastic and thermocol) should be made compulsory for


                                                            )31 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
     them.

16. Cold-drinks Lorries –Ice-Gola, Sugar cane Juice:-

16.1.The Factory must be Checked Which are sell ice –gola, coloured sarbat, kulfi and pepsi and if it is necessary to take
     sample for lab test.

16.2.The water used in ice making factories should be regularly checked.

16.3.The hawkers selling sugarcanes juice should be compelled to use disposable glasses only. They should be instructed
     to collect waste in jute bags. Care should be taken to control the nuisance of files around such places.

17. Arrangement for checking stored waters and water distribution system at places such public conference, marriage
    events or places where a large no. of people gather together should be done. They should be informed to see that
     food/eatables should not become unhygienic.

18. Care should be taken to see that water storage tanks at public places like gardens swimming pools, cinema halls,
     auditorium, bus station, colleges and other organizations Should regularly be cleaned. A record of the same should
     be maintained.

19. Information regarding water borne & water related diseases should be collected from the private practitioners desig-
    nated as sentinel centres and should be submitted to the statistical department. (Central Office)

20. Proper attention should be paid to the cleaning of open drains for the disposal of segregated solid waste, Domestic
    hazard dust and nuisance spots and also to the spraying of necessary insecticides.

21. All mutton/beef/fish/vegetable/ fruit markets should be cleaned and washed with phenyl and if uneatable items being
    sold there should be destroyed.

22. To make anti-fly activity rigorous, necessary measures should be taken for proper sanitation in all wards. The activity
    should be done keeping the breeding spots of files in mind. (Spots like Dustbin, garbage piles, cow dung heaps &
    disposable sites.)

23. Insecticides should be used generously in market areas and should maintain cleanliness to reduce breeding of flies.

24. Garbage from Municipal solid waste, disposable site, Municipal Solid Waste transfer station etc. should be regularly
    disposed off and cleanliness should be maintained and insecticide should be sprayed regularly to stop breeding of
    flies.

25. Disposal of Construction and demolition waste (C & D waste) should be done and administrative charge should be
    taken for the same.
B.   Checking of Eatable / Non Eatables Food:-
     All Food Inspector will have to make the following arrangements
1.   Inspection of organizations selling food/eatable items should be done and unhygienic items should be destroyed.
     (Action: Ch. FSO / Dy. MOH)
2.   Instructions should be issued to the organizations, Lorries/ hawkers selling food/eatable items to use disposal paper
     cups & plates only. (Action: Dy. MOH)
3.   The food /eatables sold by hawker/lorries should be inspected to maintain quality and if needed, the unhygienic food
     items should be destroyed and their selling should be stopped. (Action: Dy. MOH)


                                                           )32 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
4.   Written instructions should be given for the use of chlorinated water only for the food processing / selling units & or-
     ganization and also ice & cold drink factories. Occasional checking should be done for the purpose & water samples
     should be collected from these factories and sent to laboratory for analysis & Check-up. (Action : Ch. FSO)
5.   Written notes/warnings/notices should be issued to the owners of organization selling food/eatables for inferior quality
     as per PFA Rules & Regulations. Samples should be taken from them for this purpose. Complaints should be made in
     case of violation of any condition of the license. Implementation of licensing conditions should be ensured. Inspection
     notes should be variably be made by the inspector on his visits, in appropriate column of the license. (Action : Ch.
     FSO)
6.   To check the Hotel & Restaurant for Health & Hygienic condition & do needful action as necessary. (Action: Ch. FSO
     / Dy. MOH)
C.   Water Quality Monitoring
1.   When the complaint of contaminated water is received, Surveillance department should carry out the work in that area
     & chlorine Tablets should be distributed.
2.   Sample of the contaminated water should be taken for the lab testing from the complaining Area by Health sanitation
     wards.
3.   The complaint of contaminated water & leakage pipes should be checked by concerned department & try to solve
     that problem immediately.
4.   For the awareness of the contaminated water necessary publicity should be done.
5.   Any case of water borne & related disease observed in nearby area of urban health centre and private hospital what-
     ever action taken should be reported to head quarter.
Solid Waste Cell Section:-
1. To work according to the highest standard of cleanliness.
2. During the transportation of waste from the refuge transfer station to the final disposable site, the
waste should not fall on the road.
3. It has to be ensured that the sweeping machines used for night cleaning at all zones work smoothly.
4. It has to be ensured that there is adequate quantity of cleaning equipment at Rudrarpura depot.

Section-2
Market / Slaughter House Section:

Market superintendent will have to carry out the following activities.

1.   Regular inspection & General superintendence on Municipal Abattoirs including management and control, daily
     cleaning, disinfection and timely disposal of offal/ slaughter house waste.

2.   To carry out drive against stray cattle menace causing impairment of vehicular and pedestrian traffic in juris-
     diction of the Surat Municipal Corporation through cattle nuisance control cell (CNCC) so as to control cattle
     nuisance in the city. Initiate and take police and legal action against cattle owners for keeping their animals
     stray.

3.   Handing over ownerless cattle of municipal cattle pond to the registered Panjrapoles for their lifelong mainte-
     nance.

4.   To provide all logistic assistance and legal guidance to Dy.MOH of all zones on the matter of illegal slaughter
     of animals and unlicensed sale of meat in their jurisdiction so as to resolve the complaint satisfactorily and to
     stop this offensive act permanently.

5.   To attend all matter pertaining to the policy decision of Municipal Markets, Abattoirs, Veterinary food Hygiene
     & Public Health, Zoonosis, Stray cattle and Cattle Pond issues, Animal Birth Control Program in stray dogs
     (Sterilization and Immunize them against Rabies) etc.



Section-3
Preventive, Promotive, Curative Services :-

DUTY OF MEDICAL OFFICER OF UHC & CHC MT.HOME

On Duty Doctors of Maskati & Smimer hospitals and Medical Officer of urban health centres, Urban Health Centres
    and Maternity Homes and CHC should carry out the following activities.

1.   If more cases of water borne & water related diseases are found from any one particular area, the information
     should immediately be conveyed to the Dy.Medical Officer of Health (zone/vaccine) by phone/personally on
     the same day and a written & detail report should also be done.

2.   If dehydration is noticed in case of AGE patients they should be admitted to Maskati/Smimer/uhc/chc/50 bed
     hospital. If cholera is suspected than the stool sample of all such cases should be sent for bacteriological /
     culture tests by the hospital-medical officer. The In charge of the pathological laboratory should immediately
     send a copy of the report to the MOH .

3.   Arrangement of sufficient stock of all required drugs/ medicines to cope up with likely situations of epidemic
     should be made.

4.   Concerned medical officer should arrange to get the information from the sentinel centres (private PR actioners)
     in his area and should visit it every fortnight to get the detail status of the epidemic. Medical officer and CDPO
     of anganwadi should prepare the list of pregnant women and patients of dialysis for their respective area to be
     used at the time of disaster or at the time of flood. When informed, they should as such pregnant women and


                                                         )35 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
     patients to shift to safe place / hospital.

5.   All the medical officers should arrange for the disposal of biomedical waste, category wise, as per the Bio-
     medical waste (maintenance & handling) rules and should maintain its register regularly.

6.   Patients having symptoms like cold cough, fever, throat pain, chest pain, breathing problem and the influenza
     (H1 N1) like symptoms should be treated as required by medical officer of all health centres and should be
     categorized under the state government guidelines and if needed, patients should be referred for “throat swab”
     test in new civil/smimer hospital and the health department should be informed the same.

Epidemic control Team (Vaccine branch)will have to do the following activities.

1.   Arrangement for on the spot treatment of patient in case of possible epidemic should be made. Medical team
     including necessary doctors, nurses etc. should be kept ready. Arrangement for keeping sufficient stock of
     vaccine required for this purpose should also be made.

2.   Surveillance activities in cases of various diseases should also be arranged.

3.   Actions for health education of the public should be taken.

4.   Statistical data of various diseases should be prepared with the help of statistical departments. The health
     officer should be helped in correspondence/ coordination work to be done with the private hospitals, clinics.
     RDD office, New Civil Hospital, District Panchayat Office, District collector Officer, & Government of Gujarat.

5.   If there is complain of water contamination, than the Supervisor of the team should do chlorine test and collect
     water samples for bacteriological test.

6.   In case of fever cases the team Supervisor should take the slides, give medicines, and send the slides for
     testing to VBDC main office.

7.   In cases of hepatitis and Ac GE, the supervisor should do the reporting in prescribed format to Chief SI vac-
     cine branch. Chief SI vaccine branch should submit the reports of daily work through proper channel.

                                                     CHAPTER - 7
                               Vector Borne Diseases Control Department
Health related activities are following :
•    Active surveillance and intra Domestic And antilarval activity
•    Engineering Measures
•    Biological Measures
•    Anti Adult Work
•    Public awareness campaign
          Under the guidance of the Municipal Commissioner, cooperation and involvement of various departments should
     be accepted in order to achieve this.
Responsibilities of Dy Moh(Surveillance)/Insecticide Officer and Additional Insecticide Officer: -
1.   Overall monitoring supervision and reporting work shall be done
2.   Overall Monitoring/Supervision of the Operations of the VBDC Dept of the Zone to ensure prompt implementation of
     all control measures in view of the abnormal changes in vector born diseases and to ensure timely, effective and result
     oriented entomological, cross checking, Plague surveillance, store, central lab and reporting operations at HQ level.
Responsibilities of Assistant Insecticide Officer:-
•    The Assistant Insecticide Officer has accountability for all tasks completed within their designated area. Periodically
     supervision for effective input is necessary to make the active surveillance, anti-larval, anti-adult, and other vector-borne
     illness control efforts in the zone area successful and result-oriented. Each week, as needed, the Antilarval Supervisor
     will conduct a survey of the area designated for mosquito breeding, identify any prospective breeding sites, map them,
     and assign field workers to carry out source reduction, biological, or chemical control.
•    To evaluate the state of vector-borne diseases in the zone, work with the private hospitals, hospitals, and laboratories
     in the area to gather information, classify it, implement control measures in the impacted areas, and take the necessary
     action to produce positive outcomes.


(A) Vector Borne diseases
•    Active surveillance and intra domestic antilarval activity
1    The surveillance worker should collect slides from January to June, with a collection rate of 1.5% and a collection rate
     of 2% from July to December in the survey area.
2    The Malaria Surveillance Worker Shall record the suspected cases of malaria fever in
Every household and enter in his register. It should also sent to the medical officer of the nearest urban health centre.
3    The surveillance worker should conduct an intradomestic survey of 10,000 people on a fortnightly basis on. In all water
     bodies locations other than those utilized for drinking water, sewage must be treated.
4    Every patient who tested positive for malaria should be visited to provide RT at the recommended dosage and do fol-
     low up with the patient in accordance with the instructions, and keep track of the pertinent information.
Antilarval activity
•    Antilarval Supervisor Survey of current and potential breeding sites for mosquitoes in his or her assigned area. Source
     reduction and bio chemical control should be done to stop the breeding of mosquitoes.
Engineering Measures :-
•    Environmental manipulation like airtight closure, disposal of waste containers, fish release, fountain, open plot, it should

                                                              )37 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
     be done in coordination with the concerned engineering department of the zone.
•    Dredging, desilting operations of creeks passing through the zone area as well as open drainage
•    Cleaning of storm water inlet chamber at various S.T.P. Water treatment plant, gardening, vehicle depot, hydraulic
     depot and waste disposal activity within the time frame.
•    Dewatering operation by dewatering pumps in open plots during the monsoon season where water cannot be drained
     by soil or drains.
Biological Measures
•    More hatcheriesneed to be developed and maintained in all zone and adequate monitoring of availability of fishes to
     be ensured.
•    Permanent as well as temporary breeding spots in which carnivorous fish can live, biological control with carnivorous
     fish only and periodic monitoring.
•    Surveying in Railway Washing Yard, ST Depot, other Govt and Semi Govt Premises for prevention of vector borne
     disease control. For necessary action concern nodal officer of premises as to be contacted by AIO.
•    Operation of Guppy Fish Release in open plots during the monsoon season in plots where water cannot be drained.
Anti Adult Work
•    •Fogging operation has to be done in the area of positive cases of Malaria and Dengue detected through Jamie’s software.
•    Carrying out effective fogging in and around 50 houses of Malaria Dengue or Chikunguniya positive cases and maintaining
     their register.
•    Effective fogging and intra domestic as well as peri domestic operations should be carried out in the area related to the
     complaints received through the Municipal Sabhyashri social workers, leading citizens and grievance redressal system of
     Surat Municipal corporation.
Public awareness campaign
    In order to create public awareness for vector born diseases, IEC done in public places like colleges, schools, fairs
     and MahilaMandals where housewives are present, Anganwadis, Voluntary Organizations, NGOs, Indian Medical
     Association, In contact with general practitioners in contact with Malaria workshops should be organized. In the next
     month of June, banners and posters will have to be put up in different important public places of the city showing the
     precautionary measures taken against malaria. Advertisements should be placed in local newspapers from time to time
     with information on malaria prevention in a manner that the general public can understand.
Public awareness campaigns should be carried out in different areas of the city by means of publicity vans. Organizing
     puppet shows and street plays in slum areas and imparting information about prevention of mosquito- borne diseases.
Rodent and flea control activities
•    Trapping will have to be done in areas where there are more rodents. (Action : AIO)
•    On the next day of trapping dustings of Malathion 5 % will have to be done in rat borrows. (Action : AIO)
•    Intensive activities will have to be done in the areas having high fly index to reduce the index. (Action : AIO)
•    Samples of Viscera&Cera of the rodents will have to be sent to NICD, Delhi for testing every month and this information
     should be sent to Addl. Controller (AP.) Gandhinagar (Action : IO)
Entomological works :-
•    Entomological team should be instructed to work on information like Mosquitoes species identification, Malaria Vector
     Density, Filaria Vector Density, Aedes Vector Density, General Mosquito Density gathered from different zones.
•    All zonal heads should conduct weekly review regarding the actions taken and discuss the matters. Health officer should
     review all the work done and coordinated report should be submitted to insecticide officer.

                                                 CHAPTER - 8
                                 Extreme Heat / Heatwave / sun stroke

      Excessive heat is harmful to human health. An increase in day temperature is being observed at the begin-
  ning of this year. Cases of sun stroke due to excess heat are reported. Which if not treated on time, can prove
  to be fatal.


    In cases of sun stroke, the body temperature is usually high. As the temperature of the environment is high,
  sweating is very high and due to this the body temperature cannot decrease, which has adverse effects on the
  health of a person.
• Muscle weakness or cramps
• extreme thirst
• dizziness or fainting;
• nausea or vomiting;
• rapid breathing
• fast heartbeat


For general population
• Avoid strenuous activities when outside in the afternoon
• Wear thin loose, cotton garments preferably light coloured.
• Children, Pregnant women and elderly are most at risk of heatstroke.
• Drink Lots of Water. Keep electrolytes (water with salt and sugar) at home.
• Use fan, spray bottles, damp cloths, ice towels to cool down body.
• Avoid Starvation as much as possible during hot season.
• If someone has a heatstroke, cool him/her with an ice pack and cold water, then rush him/her to hospital.
• Ensuring that people are told to be cautious as per heat wave alerts sent from the state level.
• In addition to this, Ensuring that the quantity of necessary medicines like iv fluids, ice packs, ors and other life
  saving medicines and other necessary equipment are available in sufficient quantity and ensuring that adequate
  drinking water system and cooling is maintained in every health facility.
• To encourage installation of solar panels, cooling devices and roofs, window shades, rain water harvesting system
  etc. where possible in urban health center.
• Follow public Health Advisory : Extreme Heat/Heat wave(Do’s and Don’t)


Stay hydrated:
• Drink sufficient water whenever possible, even if you are not thirsty. Thirst is not a good indicator of dehydration.
• Carry drinking water when travelling

                                                         )41 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
• Use Oral Rehydration Solution (ORS), and consume homemade drinks like lemon water, butter milk/lassi, fruit
  juices with some added salt.
• Eat seasonal fruits and vegetables with high water content like watermelon, muskmelon, orange, grapes, pine-
  apple, cucumber, lettuce or other locally available fruits and vegetables.
Stay covered:
• Wear thin, loose, cotton garments preferably light coloured
• Cover your head: use umbrella, hat, cap, towel and other traditional head gears during exposure to direct sunlight
•   Wear shoes or chappals while going out in sun


Stay alert:
• Listen to Radio; watch TV; read Newspaper for local weather news. Get the latest update of weather on India
  Meteorological Department (IMD) website at https://mausam.imd.gov.in.
• Stay indoors/in shade as much as possible: • In well ventilated and cool places • Block direct sunlight and heat
  waves: Keep windows and curtains closed during the day, especially on the sunny side of your house. Open
  them up at night to let cooler air in. • If going outdoor, limit your outdoor activity to cooler times of the day i.e.,
  morning and evening • Reschedule or plan outdoor activities during cooler parts of the day.
• For vulnerable population although anyone at any time can suffer from the heat stress & heat-related illness,
  some people are at greater risk than others and should be given additional attention. These include: • Infants and
  young children • People working outdoors • Pregnant women • People who have a mental illness • Physically ill,
  especially with heart disease or high blood pressure • Travellers from colder climate to a hot climate should allow
  one week’s time for their bodies to acclimatized to heat, avoid overexertion, and should drink plenty of water. Ac-
  climatization is achieved by gradual increase (over 10-15days) in exposure/physical activity in hot environment.
• Other precautions
•   Elderly or sick people living alone should be supervised and their health monitored on a daily basis.
•   Keep your home cool, use curtains, shutters or sunshade and open windows at night.
•   Try to remain on lower floors during the day.
•   Use fan, spray bottles, damp cloths, ice towels to cool down body.
• Immersing feet in 20°C water above ankle provides rapid cooling by reducing dehydration and thermal discomfort.
•   Avoid getting out in the sun, especially between 12:00 noon and 03:00 pm
•   Avoid strenuous activities when outside in the afternoon
• Do not go out barefoot
•   Avoid cooking during peak summer hours. Open doors and windows to ventilate cooking area adequately
• Avoid alcohol, tea, coffee and carbonated soft drinks or drinks with large amount of sugar- as these actually,
  lead to loss of more body fluid or may cause stomach cramps
• Do not eat stale food.
•   Do not leave children or pets in parked vehicle. Temperature inside a vehicle could get dangerous.
•   Caution workers to avoid direct sunlight • Provide shaded work area for workers. Temporary shelter can be cre-
    ated at work site.


                                                          )42 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
•   Schedule strenuous and outdoor jobs to cooler times of the day i.e., morning and evening hours.
• I ncrease the frequency and length of rest breaks for outdoor activities- at least every 5 minutes after 1 hour of
  Laboure work
•   Precautions During Mass gathering/Sport event :-
• Outdoor/indoor crowded situations increase risk of acute heat-related illnesses (HRI) even in absence of active
    heatwave alerts in the area.
• Physical exertion, direct sun exposure, overcrowding, and difficult access to water, food and shade may worsen
    health in vulnerable groups.
•   Attendees should stay hydrated, cool, be aware of HRI signs, symptoms and seek medical care.


For Employers and worker :-
Provide cool drinking water at work place and remind them to drink a cup of water every 20minutes or more fre-
    quently to stay hydrated
• Caution workers to avoid direct sunlight
• Provide shaded work area for workers. Temporary shelter can be created at work site.
• Schedule strenuous and outdoor jobs to cooler times of the day i.e., morning and evening hours
• Increase the frequency and length of rest breaks for outdoor activities- at least every 5 minutes after 1 hour of
  labour work
• Listen to Radio; watch TV; read Newspaper for local weather news and act accordingly. Get the latest update of
  weather on India Meteorological Department (IMD) website at https://mausam.imd.gov.in/
• Assign additional workers or slow down the pace of work
• Make sure everyone is properly acclimatized: it takes weeks to acclimatize to a hotter climate. Do not work for
  more than three hours in one day for the first five days of work. Gradually increase the amount and time of work.
• Train workers to recognize factors which may increase the risk of developing a heat related illness and the signs
  and symptoms of heat stress and start a “buddy system” since people are not likely to notice their own symptoms
• Trained First Aid providers should be available and an emergency response plan should be in place in the event
  of a heat-related illness.
• Pregnant workers and workers with a medical condition or those taking certain medications should discuss with
  their physicians about working in the heat.
• If working outdoors wear light-coloured clothing preferably long sleeve shirt and pants, and cover the head to
  prevent exposure to direct sunlight.
• Organize awareness campaigns for employees
• Install temperature and forecast display at the workplace.
• Distribute informational pamphlets and organize training for employers and workers regarding health impacts of
  extreme heat and recommendations to protect themselves during high temperature

   The following facilities are available at various Urban Health Centres, Urban Health Centres and
Maternity Homes, Urban Collective Health Centres and 50 Bed Hospitals of Surat Municipal Corpora-
tion.
   Services and facilities provided at Urban Health Centre :-
   • General OPD treatment
   • Laboratory testing
   • Operation of anti-rabies vaccine
   • Diagnosis and treatment of malaria
   • Screening and treatment of TB
   • Screening and treatment of leprosy
   • Screening, counselling and treatment of STD (Sexually Transmitted Diseases)
   • Screening and treatment of non-communicable diseases
   • OPD treatment on fixed days of the week by various disease specialists (Honorary Specialists)
   • Planning and implementation of various national programmes

   • Registration, examination and treatment of pregnant women, examination and treatment of ex-
pectant mothers under NHM - RCH II program (with assistance as decided under various schemes of
Govt.)
   • Universal Immunization Program: Immunization of pregnant women and children up to 16 years of
age as per National Immunization Schedule
   • Vitamin A Programme: Action to provide twice yearly dose of Vitamin A to eligible children up to 5
years of age.
   • Family Planning Programme: Counselling and facilitation of adoption of various methods of family
planning (with assistance as determined under various schemes of Govt.)
   • National Child Health Programme: Health screening and treatment of children from birth to 18
years of age
   • PMJAY Program: Under this program card making operation of eligible beneficiaries as per the
guidelines of Govt.

  Services and facilities provided at Urban Health Centre and Maternity Home: -
  • 30 bed admission facility provided for Indoor patient.
  Services and facilities provided at Urban Community Health Centre:

   In addition to the services and facilities provided at the Urban Collective Health Centre above 04
(Four) 50 Bed Hospitals.
   • 50 bed admission facility
   • Full time diagnosis and treatment by gynaecologist and paediatrician
   • Dental and physiotherapy services
   • Physicians and surgeons have been appointed and the services will start soon.

                                         50 BEDS HOSPITAL
                50 bed hospital With the Noble intention of providing quality health care facilities to the
citizens of Surat, Surat Municipal Corporation inaugurated a 50-bed hospital.

      Surat Municipal Corporation started 50 bed hospitals at given Below. At 50 beds hospital services
of OPD and IPD treatment. Specialist Treatment like medicine, surgery, gynaecologist, paediatrics, phy-
sician, physiotherapist, dentist and Emergency services are available.
