                                                 CHAPTER - 10
                Standing Instructions for actions to be taken During Monsoon
                               (From First rain or 15th of June)
    The Divisional Heads, Departmental Heads and all Zonal Officers of SMC should take below mentioned actions
in case of emergency such as Heavy Rains Flood, Cyclone and other Natural Calamities.

Work of Control Room :
1. The Central Wireless Control Room at The ICCC (Integrated Command and Control Center) be set for the
   working as the control room to perform the activities to cope with flood, cyclone, heavy rain etc. City Engineer
   will be incharged.

2.   Every zone should start a Control Room to perform the activities to cope with the emergency such as flood,
     cyclone and natural calamities. This information will have to send to the Central Wireless Control Room The
     ICCC (Integrated Command and Control Center).

3.   No leave of any employee should be sanctioned except in case of special case or illness during emergencies.

4.   At the time of emergency, all Deputy Engineers, Assistant Engineer, Junior Engineers, Dy. Medical Officer of
     Health, Chief Sanitary Inspectors, Section Officers etc & other responsible officers / employees will have to
     remain present at concerned zone offices. The officers / employees listed here with will have to be present
     at the H.Q. and staff of the primary education committee at the designated location in the statement. (list or
     annexure )

5.   Central departments like Traffic, Hydraulic, Drainage and Road Department etc will have to speedily provide
     whatever help and guidance demanded by the zone offices for their routine work.

6.   A latest list of the names, addresses along with phone no., fax & mobile no. of offices associated with emer-
     gency like flood, cyclone and heavy rain will have to be kept ready at the central control room and every zone
     control room.

7.   To inform the public immediately about the safety measures and rescue and relief operations in times of emer-
     gency, the arrangement for the ‘public address systems ‘ vehicle will have to be done on zone basis.

8.   The effects of flood in the creeks and in the river are in different proportions in different zones. Keeping this in
     mind, it is necessary to make staff, equipments, vehicles, machinery etc available from other zone areas for
     the affected area completing the activities during and after the floods within time limit prescribed, as a part of
     preplanning to be done. ( the following should be done )

     I.During the flood in the creeks two teams from Central Zone will have to be allotted to Udhna Zone for the
     work (to be done) while during river flood two teams form Udhna Zone will have to be allotted to Central Zone.

     II.At the time of creek flood two teams will have to be alloted form Katargam zone to Limbayat Zone for the work
     to be done. While incase of river flood, two teams from Limbayat Zone will have to be alloted to the Katargam
     Zone.

     III.During creek flood, one team out of the two from Rander zone should be alloted to Athwa Zone area and the
     other team to Varachha Zone area while during river flood (vice versa) one team each from Athwa & Varachha

                                                          )64 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
     zones should be alloted to Rander zone.
     IV. In every team for rescue, relief, cleanliness, rehabilitation etc work the officer / employees as under should
     be included.
             (a) One Deputy Engineer.
             (b) Two Jr./Assitt. Engineer.
             (c) Four Technical Assist.
             (d) Twenty Beldar with equipments.
             (e) One Chief S. I.
             (f) Two S.I. / S.S.I.
             (g) Three Mukadams (Foremen)
             (h) Twenty five cleaners with equipments.
     V.For every team necessary vehicles and machinery will have to be made available.
9.   City Engineer will have to work in coordination with District Collector and State Government officials.

10. Copies of the reports of daily work done by zone offices / fire brigade / health dept. will have to be submitted
    to central control room.

11. Public Relations Officer will have to do all the activities related to co-ordinated press briefing, transmission,
    photography etc under the direct control of Dy.Commissioner.

12. The report of the daily activities done by concerned zone / fire brigade departments & health department
    will have to be sent to the Public Relations Officer. On the basis of these reports, PRO has to prepare the
    press note under the instruction of Dy.Commissioner.

Rescue Wrok :
13. The Fire & Emergency department will have to arrange in advance for installing wireless set and boats
    near the slums on the banks of the creeks, river and other slums likely to submerge under (flood) water.

14. The concerned zone and Fire and Emergency Services Dept. will have to carry out rescue and relief
    work at the time of flood, cyclone, or heavy rains. The coordination of such work will have to be made
    by divisional head of the Fire & Emergency Services department.

15. At the time of floods / cyclone, arrangement for boats and siren will have to be made by Chief Fire Of-
    ficer in advance.

Relief work :
16. Every zonal officer will have to arrange for providing schools for relief centres. Chief Security Officer
    will have to arrange for security staff for emergency.

17. The Administrative Officer -MSB will have to provide the staff of primary education committee for open-
    ing the schools to work as relief centers. The zone will have to arrange for cleaning the schools after
    the relief work is over. Arrangement for providing security staff during this period will have to be done
    by Chief Security Officer.

18. During heavy rains, flood & cyclone relief and rescue operations, preparation and distribution of food
    packages along with cleaning of relief centres and health oriented activities in affected areas will have
    to be carried out by concerned Zonal Officer / Dy. Health Officer and Assessment and Recovery Officer.



                                                         )65 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
Rehabilitation work :
19. All Zone Officers and Dy. Health Officers should carry out activities in their concerned areas such as scraping,
    filling of the pits / trenches, patch work, lifting of solid waste, repairing of leakage of water & drainage lines,
    spraying of insecticides, distribution of medicines / drugs, fogging, oil spraying etc. Besides these, removal
    of obstructing encroachment / structures for the disposal of storm water will have to be done. Other activities
    such as removal of obstructing building materials, destruction of unhygienic food material etc. will also to be
    done. Preventive measure will have to be taken so that epidemic does not spread.

20. All Zonal Officers will have to see that the complaints forwarded by central wireless control room from their
    concerned areas and complaints made from the leading citizens / newspapers or enlightened organizations
    are redressed at the earliest. Controlled vigilance will have to be kept to see that epidemic does not spread.

21. Zonal officers are authorized to hire at the approved rate of rent, additional trucks, tempo, tractors, bulldozers,
    loaders & such other vehicles for ten days needed at the time of emergency.

22. Store Superintendent will have to keep enough stock of petromex, tagara, spades / pawada, ropes, galvanized
    sheets etc at affected places, for use during emergency.

23. Every zonal officer will have to keep sufficient stock of petromex, tagaras, spades / pawada, ropes, gl.sheets,
    vali sand, rubble, chhara, sand bags, road material etc for the affected areas.

24. Prior arrangement for the availability of premix material of bitumen from the patch department of the zone will
    have to be made.

25. Every zonal officer will have to look after the activities of road repair work.

26. Garden Superintendent will have to arrange for lifting the trees fallen during heavy rains / cyclone.

27. Provision should also be made for air-tight overhead/under ground tanks in building under construction.

28. Lifting & Disposal of big animal carcasses should be handled by solid waste management at main office where
    of for small animal carcasses should be done by respective zones .

29. Filarial/Malaria Branch of the zones should take immediate extensive action to control Filarial/Malaria in af-
    fected areas and Insecticide office should supervise on all such activity.

    The above instructions should be strictly followed. Orders from necessary officers should be made at zone
    level and to concerned persons will have to be made known about these in advance.




                                                         )66 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq

qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
                                                  CHAPTER - 11
                The Standing Instructions given for Preparedness about flood,
                               cyclone to department / Zones
            [At emergent time when there is a forecasting (about - flood / cyclone)]
     The Standing Instructions given by Commissioner for starting the activities regarding possibility of cyclone / rain.

Rescue Operations :
1. Garden Departments should keep the equipment, tractors ready along with the staff for cutting the trees.

2.    Check up whether all the vehicles / equipments of fire brigade are kept in working condition or not.

3.    Contact the NGOs; ask them to keep the volunteers ready.

4.    The keys of relief centres / schools should be obtained. Be ready in advance for the transfer of the people of the
      affected areas keeping in mind heavy rains & tidal waves. Arrange for vehicles for publishing messages in all zones.

5.    The Town Planner and all the Zonal Officer should inform the concerned agencies for removing all the hoard-
      ings put on the roads.

6.    All the Zonal Officers should take precautionary measures by keeping necessary staff ready for continuous
      discharge (disposal) of water from the low lying areas likely to have accumulation of water due to nearby creeks
      having inflow of water due to tides.

7.    When forecast of severe cyclone and heavy rain is made, all Zonal Officers should take necessary steps so
      that because of such calamities deaths / accidents do not take place.

8.    All Zonal Officers should arrange for keeping generators in working condition at important places.

9.    In every meeting held under their command / control they should go on reminding about the disaster manage-
      ment plan prepared regarding flood & cyclone.

Relief Operations :

10. All zones control room and central control room should be continued till the receipt of further instructions.

11. No officer / employee should leave the head quarters.

12. Contingency plan should be kept hardly and relief operations should be carried out accordingly.

13. The staff, equipments etc should be kept ready for relief work in every zone.

14. Day Off’s, Holiday (leave) of all Fire Brigade staff should be cancelled and they should be given alert message.

15. Whenever there is a possibility of cyclone to blow at a speed of 100 km per hour & this speed is likely to go up
    to 150 km per hour, serious preparations should be made. (for rescue & other activities )

16. Whenever there is a possibility of heavy rains preparations should be so made that rescue & relief operations
    can be started immediately.

17. Alert message should be given to the staff so that all of them can be present at short notice.

                                                           )83 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
18. Whenever the cyclone forecast is continuing all Zonal Officers should continue their activities of precautionary
    measures in the concerned areas entrusted to them.

19. Arrangement should be made to maintain the message properly in the Log book at the central control room &
    zone control rooms during the cyclone / flood / natural calamity.

20. Sometimes difficulties in the areas in the city due to some matters in the areas adjoining the city. All the Zonal Of-
    ficers should take these into account and should take necessary steps through the nearby zone regarding them.

Aimed at Health :

21. For treatment during relief work, hospital, medicine etc and also the emergency staff of the hospital should be
    kept ready.


22. The medical van of the Surat Municipal Corporation should be kept ready. The concerned doctors should be
    kept in touch and should be told to remain present on (short notice) information.

23. For additional patients necessary faculties should be kept ready in the municipal hospitals & health centres.

24. Immediate after the water recedes the arrangement for cleaning the mud & dirt form the low lying slum areas
    due to accumulation of water during tides should be made by all Zonal Officers.

Enlightenment of People :

25. In concerned zones, all Zonal Officers should inform concerned Municipal Councilors and office bearers of
    NGOs regarding the concerned emergency (which has occurred)

26. In order that the public comes to know regarding precautionary measures to be taken at the time of cyclone,
    the PRO should publish the matter through the newspapers and local TV channel.

Precautionary Measures :

27. At the time of cyclone, The Garden department officers should keep in contact with contractors who are given
    the contract of cutting the trees & inform them to keep their men ready.

28. Deputy Eng. / Ex. Eng. (street light) and all Zonal Officers should take steps so that there is no loss / loss of
    public life due to the light poles.

29. Brackets of the high mast towers in the city should be taken down as precautionary measures.

30. The Garden Superintendent should take into account the forecast of cyclone and should arrange for a survey
    of old, likely to fall down and risky trees and take precautionary measures.




                                                          )84 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
                                                    CHAPTER - 12
                            Flood in the Creeks: Information & Management

   Mainly Varachha creeks, Koyali creeks, Mithi creeks, Kankra creek, Bhedvad creek, Chhaprabhatha creek etc
pass through the Surat city. Their discharge to sea is through main creek and Mindhoela river. The water of Tena
creek flows directly into the sea. In the upper flow there are taluka areas of Bardoli, Kamrej, Mahuwa (part) and
Palsana.

    The catchments area / length of the various creeks passing through Surat city is as under.
    Sr.No.        Name of the creek           Catchment area (sq.km)             Length (km)          Creek Length (km)
      1               Koyali Creek                        13.25                       8.35                     8.35
      2                Mithi Creek                       154.05                       53.28                    6.50
      3               Kankra Creek                       226.42                       8.15                     8.15
      4             Bhedwad Creek                         40.50                       15.00                    11.01
      5             Varachha Creek                        85.54                       24.57                    5.07
      6          Chhaprabhatha Creek                      26.86                       7.50                     6.205
      7                Tena Creek                         25.91                       6.84                        -
      8               Sonari Creek                        71.77                       21.28                    7.94
   Due to (i) heavy rain in the catchments areas of these creeks flow of water increases (ii) local rain / flood in
the parent river etc as also (iii) there is lack of disposal of water in the creeks due to the effect of tides in the sea,
the water in the creeks over flow in the following low lying areas and areas of societies around as listed below are
generally affected.
    East Zone (varachha) - Areas on the outskirts of Nana Varachha, Kapodra, Karanj.
    South East Zone ( Limbayat) - Magob, Dumbhal, Kamrunagar, Mithikhadi, Ratanjinagar, Krantinagar, Raonagar,
     Sanjaynagar, Govindnagar, Salimnagar, Mandarwaje low cost colony, 181/184 Bhathena and low lying area of
     Anjana Chimni Tekra.
    South Zone (Udhana) - Sanjaynagar, premnagar.
    South West (Athwa) - Rasulabad, Azadnagar, Santoshinagar, Indiranagar.
    North Zone (Katargam) - Amroli, Ganeshpura- Kosad - GHB colony, Kosad village area, motavarachha.
   In addition to this, if heavy rain falls in the city area during this period, care should be taken that enough water
does not flow out through the storm water drainage line laid down by Surat Municipal Corporation and as such in
some low lying area of the city, there may be water accumulation or increase in the level of the accumulated water.
    Due to high tide waves, sea water enters these creeks whereas generally creeks direct by dispose off the water into the sea.
   This Disaster Management Preparedness Plan includes most of the preplanning and necessary steps to be
taken during the disasters situation that arises due to heavy rain, flood, cyclone in the areas in the banks of the
above creeks in the city for seeing that there should not be loss of life & property of the people or it should be the
least and after that for carrying out rescue & relief operations for establishing the normal condition and there after
doing necessary activities of rehabilitation.
    All the areas around khadi and low line areas were affected by heavy rains in Surat and District in 1998 July. After
     that during beginning of Aug-2013 & end of June-2005 due to heavy rains in catchments areas in the districts.
    In order that the information about upper catchments areas & of rains is continuously available & is continu-
     ously monitored, it should be provided in the form attached herewith to the central control & all zonal controls.


                                                             )85 )
qq DISASTER MANAGEMENT AND PREPAREDNESS PLAN qq
     For the purpose the concerned persons should remain in contact with Taluka / district control rooms of the
     collectorate.
 For monitoring of the level of water & is continuously between Bhathena, Jivanjyot & South Zone Office and under
  the bridge on Mithi Creek & for the readings of the level under the bridge near Limbayat (Mithi Creek), Bhedwad
  creek, kankra creek & koyali creek near the south zone office and on the bridges on other creeks marking / paint-
  ing taking into account GTS, every two hours reading should be taken and filling the details of water levels of all
  the creeks in the attached form it should be submitted to the central control for their information.
    The concerned zone should arrange for proper information of floods in the creeks to the public so that people
     can shift to safe places in appropriate time.
Form of daily monitoring

                                     CHAPTER - 13
                                TAPI Flood Control Scheme


    Under the “TAPI River Flood Control Scheme”, Surat Manicipal Corporation has Con-
                struct The Flood Control Stucture as below in surat City.

   1.To prevent the flood in the lower level areas of Varachha, Flood control structure
(Sluics Regulator) has been built on Varachha Khadi.

   2.To Prevent the flood in the lower level areas of Chhaprabhatha, the flood control
structure (Sluice Regulator), has been built On Chhaprabhatha Khadi.

                                                   (199)
                        At the time of Disaster: Do this necessary
                                        Earthquake

       Earthquake is a type of natural disaster. Earthquakes occur due to abnormal
movements in the upper layers of the earth. Generally: Any disaster is forecasted and
advance preparations are made to prevent the disaster and reduce the loss of life due
to the disaster to a great extent. Whereas in the case of earthquake, it is not possible to
predict. Due to which advance preparation cannot be done. The immediate aftermath of
an earthquake is a very delicate time. The operations related to the disasters caused by
the earthquake are very difficult. First of all rescue the victims / trapped people. It is
necessary to carry out the work of providing first aid to the injured and taking the
affected to a safe place if necessary, to the hospital. It is necessary to raise a relief and
rescue team on a permanent basis in an emergency like an earthquake. So in case of
storm we have made advance preparations like rescue and relief teams, heavy
machinery, essential items etc. It is very important to keep it handy. In the case of
earthquakes, the second and third post-disaster phases play a very important role. In
the case of an earthquake, the first phase focuses on immediate treatment of the
affected, disposal of dead bodies, prevention of the spread of epidemics, etc. While in
the second phase, it is necessary to provide facilities like food grains, water and shelter
to the affected people in coordination with all government departments and social
voluntary organizations. In the third phase, permanent resettlement works and provision
of cash assistance are to be done for the affected. The magnitude of the earthquake
can be known from the Richter scale. The Richter scale can be used to determine the
effect of the damages on the Richter scale score. In addition to the effect of damage on
the Richter scale, an idea of the damage caused by an earthquake on the Richter scale
is obtained from the scale mentioned below.

       Apart from this, the Do's & Don'ts for people to do at the time of earthquake are
as per the attached picture.

        should be done accordingly to minimise casualties.

        and guidance regarding the calamity, publicity/transmission should be done.

                                                To survive heat stroke/sun
                                                  stroke during summer
 DOs                                                                                               DON’Ts
 •   Wear cotton clothes in summer.
 •   Use caps, umbrella and glasses so that it can help covering the face.
                                                                                                   • Do not eat trite food sold in market.
 •   If you happen to go outside make sure the entire body including the head is covered.
 •   Avoid direct sun light and stand under a tree with a shade and coolness.                      • Avoid use of ice sold in market.
 •   Drink ample amount of water and also drink lemon juice if possible.
 •   Cover head with wet cloth and if required use wet cloth on body to keep the body cool.        • Avoid eating items made of milk or
 •   Kids, pregnant women, elders and patients should take extra care in sunshine.                   pulp in wedding functions.
 •   MixPalash (Kesudo) flowers or neem leaves in water for giving bath to kids.
 •   In case of headache, restlessness, dizziness, nausea or fever consult the doctor of nearest
                                                                                                   • Do not remain starved in summer.
     hospital and take necessary treatment.
